Results › Probabilistic programs: Dice, Alea.jl, Roulette and further systems › Bit-blasted programs

bb_spacex_p4

The programs of the bit-blasting paper (Garg, Holtzen, Van den Broeck and Millstein, PLDI 2024) on ladders of precision. Alea.jl runs each program itself; tddlog runs the circuit Alea.jl builds for it, and Roulette the circuit's Dice program translated to Racket. 600 s a run. Geni, noDice, Psi, Genfer, SPPL, Pluck, PITA, aspmc, Storm, PRISM, Ace, Merlin, pyAgrum, pgmpy, libDAI and TensorInference.jl ran too, each on the programs it takes (its own paper's, the Dice programs, the ProbLog programs or the networks); a system's time is its process's, start-up included. Dice, Roulette, Roulette's process, ProbLog, Geni, noDice, Psi, Genfer, SPPL, Pluck, PITA, aspmc, Storm, PRISM, Ace, Merlin, pyAgrum, pgmpy, libDAI and TensorInference.jl ran in a later run, on other machines, on the instances the release run did not give them, each beside tddlog: their times compare with that run's tddlog, not with the other columns, and the comparisons below use it. 27 more instances of the ladders no system ran: each system had failed on a smaller size. The circuit of the Alea.jl program data/alea/hybit/spacex.jl with pieces=4.

Seconds, a time limit of 600 s; >600: the time limit ran out. memory: out of memory. A process time is the whole process's, with the language's start and compilation; the other time of the same system leaves them out, as its paper times it. A time is the median of the repetitions below, a failed one counted as infinite. A system run in several forms (ProbLog's knowledge compilers, Ace's routes) has the time of its fastest form.

The programs

The files each system was handed, byte for byte: written by the release's scripts at the run's commit and captured by running the benchmark driver's own code with no system started. The first tab is the original program as its authors wrote it; each other tab is a translation of it into the language of the systems that read it, and says which script wrote it.

The original: the program as its authors wrote it, handed to Alea.jl as it is.

Read by Alea.jl.

Alea.jl
julia scripts/alea_bench.jl run --warm-below 200 --set pieces=4 data/alea/hybit/spacex.jl
data/alea/hybit/spacex.jl827 bytes, 20 linessha256 ec123a56eddbfed132945129c748d4debd86777dfbc6df3aaabdab16179d503e

In the repository (the baselines branch): data/alea/hybit/spacex.jl.

# spacex (canyon289 2022): the expected completion time of a rocket, a sum
# of three normal durations. Bit-blasting paper (Garg, Holtzen, Van den
# Broeck, Millstein, PLDI 2024), Table 2: 19 bits, 32 pieces. From
# Tractables/Alea.jl hybrid/benchmarks/spacex/spacex.jl at cf5e5b8 (Apache
# License 2.0), its body unchanged.
using Alea, Distributions

const QUERY = :expectation
const bits = 11
const pieces = 32
const DFiP = DistFix{8+bits, bits}

model() = @alea begin
    engines = bitblast(DFiP, Normal(5, 1), pieces, 0.0, 64.0)
    first_stage = bitblast_exponential(DFiP, Normal(10, 3), pieces, 0.0, 64.0)
    completed_first_stage = engines + first_stage
    second_stage = bitblast_exponential(DFiP, Normal(15, 3), pieces, 0.0, 64.0)
    completed_rocket = completed_first_stage + second_stage
    completed_rocket
end

Psi (later run)
Psi answers the Dice programs exactly, one run per component of the program's value, the times added. On its own linear regression it answers the posterior means of the continuous model, E(a) and E(b), which the circuit's bit queries do not have, so there is nothing to compare them with.
